will make all the minus terms which have greater than two
\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{p}
terms in it disappear, which leads us to the final form:

\partial_{t}f_{W}(\bm{x},\bm{p})=\left[\frac{p^{2}}{2ma^{2}}+V(\bm{x})\right]\frac{2}{\hbar}\sin\left(\frac{\hbar}{2}(\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{x}\vec{\partial}_{p}-\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{p}\vec{\partial}_{x})\right)f_{W}(\bm{x},\bm{p}).

(D.52)

Defining the
Moyal
bracket for two functions on phase-space

\left\{f(\bm{x},\bm{p}),g(\bm{x},\bm{p})\right\}_{\rm MB}^{\hbar}=\frac{2}{\hbar}f\sin\left(\frac{\hbar}{2}(\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{x}\vec{\partial}_{p}-\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle\partial\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\partial\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{p}\vec{\partial}_{x})\right)g.

(D.53)

we can recast this evolution equation as

\partial_{t}f_{W}=\left\{\mathscr{H},f_{W}\right\}_{\rm MB}^{\hbar}=\left\{\mathscr{H},f_{W}\right\}_{\rm PB}+\order{\hbar^2}

(D.54)

where
\left\{\cdot,\cdot\right\}_{\rm PB}
is the standard Poisson bracket.

There is a whole branch of mathematics which deals with the Moyal bracket and how unique such structures are on Poissonian manifolds called
deformation quantisation
. For the purposes of this thesis however, the Moyal bracket in Equation
D.53
is the only one which we will consider.

Appendix E
Appendix for Chapter
8

E.1
Effect of larger
\hbar_{\rm PPT}

Figure
E.1
shows the power spectrum and reduced skewness as measured from density fields produced using 1LPT/1PPT via
monofonIC
as described in Chapter
8
. Using a value of
\hbar_{\rm PPT}=2.2\,h^{-2}\ \rm Mpc^{2}
(twice the value used in the main analysis of Chapter
8
), it is possible to produce skewness enhancements similar to those due to changing the initial conditions through wave dynamics alone (compare the yellow/triangle solid line to the blue/circle or orange/square dashed lines). This indicates more need to understand the appropriate mapping between
\hbar_{\rm PPT}
and FDM effective mass for setting initial conditions. However, even in this large
\hbar_{\rm PPT}
case, we see that suppressing the initial conditions does continue to enhance the skewness similarly to in the cases considered in the main Chapter.

Figure E.1
:
(Left panel) Same as Figure
8.2
but with addtional
\hbar_{\rm PPT}
which is twice the size of the one used in the main analysis. (Right panel) Same as Figure
8.4
with the additional
\hbar_{\rm PPT}
. The larger value of
\hbar_{\rm PPT}=2.2\ h^{-2}\ \rm Mpc^{2}
is able to produce the skewness of the LPT + FDM ICs case even on cold initial conditions.
